# Claude Code session log – Task 17: polish – prefetch, code splitting, performance, resilience, parity

Converted from the session transcript (JSONL): `/export` is a user command that Claude Code cannot run itself.
Session time range (UTC): 2026-11-23T18:24:51.186Z → 2026-11-23T20:16:12.836Z.

How to read this log:
- It contains the user messages, Claude's replies and tool calls with **shortened** inputs and outputs.
- Claude's internal thinking and the harness's `<system-reminder>` context blocks are omitted.
- Attached task files are shortened to a preview; the full text is in `docs/tasks/`.
- Automated notifications (background tasks) and subagent hand-backs are labelled "System / agent message": they are not typed by the user.
- Secrets (connection strings, `npg_` tokens, Neon hosts, `.env` values), e-mail addresses (except reserved test domains such as `example.com`), the Windows user name and the Vercel team name are replaced with `[REDACTED]` / `[USER]`.
- It covers the whole session up to the conversion of this log (opening PR #20 and its CI result included).

## User · 2026-11-23T18:24:51.186Z

> Addendum to Task 17:
> - Swipe gesture for the home carousel on touch devices (pointer events, no new dependency; horizontal threshold, does not block vertical scrolling).
> - Mobile LCP is 5.0 s because the hero image URL arrives only after bundle → /api/homepage → /api/blocks/hero. Shorten that chain without hardcoding content in the frontend: e.g. /api/homepage may include a small server-provided "preload" hint (the first hero slide's image srcset) that the app turns into an early <link rel="preload" as="image" imagesrcset=…>, and/or start the hero block request in parallel. Measure before/after.

```
$ cat > docs/tasks/TASK_17_polish.md <<'EOF'
# Task 17 – Polish: instant navigation, performance, resilience, final parity pass

**Model:** Opus · **Effort:** high
Start after Task 15 (homepage) is merged. No migration.

## Goal
The app should feel instant and never show a blank or broken screen. Finish with one consistent pass over every page.

## Decisions (made by the owner – do not change)
1. **`PrefetchLink`** – one shared link component used for **every internal link** (header, nav, platform row, tiles, "View all", product cards, "Other versions", breadcrumbs, orders, footer internal links):
   - prefetch on **hover after ~80 ms** (cancel if the pointer leaves earlier), on **focus**, and on **touchstart** (phones have no hover);
   - prefetches the **data** the target page needs through the existing TanStack queries: `/product/:slug` → product query; `/store?…` → listing query with the parsed params (same sanitizer as the Store page); `/orders` → orders query only when signed in; unknown targets → nothing;
   - also prefetches the target route's **code chunk** (see 2);
   - never prefetches the same thing twice within `staleTime`; respects `navigator.connection.saveData` (no prefetch then).
   Replace the existing ad-hoc card/suggestion prefetch with it where it fits (keep behaviour). Tests.
2. **Route code splitting:** keep Home, Store and Product in the main bundle; lazy-load cart, checkout/payment, orders, order detail, login, register (`React.lazy` / route `lazy`) with a skeleton fallback. Report bundle sizes before/after.
3. **Performance pass** (Lighthouse **mobile**, production build, on Home, Store, a Product page): report LCP, CLS, TBT before/after and fix the top issues, e.g. `preconnect` to `images.igdb.com`, preload the two Metropolis weights used above the fold, `font-display: swap`, correct `sizes`/`srcset`, no layout shifts. Target: CLS < 0.1, LCP < 2.5 s on Home and Store (state the measuring conditions).
4. **Resilience:** a route-level **error boundary** (`errorElement`) with a friendly page ("Something went wrong" + Retry + Home) – a render error must never white-screen the app; the global "Waking up the server…" notice still works.
5. **SPA accessibility:** on route change move focus to the page's `h1` (or a skip target) and announce the new page title; a visible **"Skip to content"** link. Every page has a unique document title.
6. **Final parity pass:** screenshots of every page at 1440 and 390 (home, store, store with search, p
… [truncated; original 4284 chars]
```

```
$ SP="C:/Users/[USER]/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/27133936c4ab5668/27828732-4b43-4175-8312-75c91eb74045/scratchpad"; curl -s -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}\n" http://127.0.0.1:4197/store; cat > "$SP/tools/lh.mjs" <<'EOF'
// Scratchpad-only: Lighthouse (mobile preset = default: Moto G Power emulation, simulated slow 4G, 4x CPU slowdown)
// on several URLs, N runs each, prints the median LCP / CLS / TBT / FCP / score per URL as JSON.
// usage: node lh.mjs <label> <runs> <url> [url...]
import lighthouse from "lighthouse";
import * as chromeLauncher from "chrome-launcher";
import fs from "node:fs";

const [label, runsArg, ...urls] = process.argv.slice(2);
const runs = Number(runsArg);
const chromePath = "C:/Program Files (x86)/Microsoft/Edge/Application/msedge.exe";
const median = (xs) => { const s = [...xs].sort((a, b) => a - b); return s[Math.floor(s.length / 2)]; };
const out = {};
for (const url of urls) {
  const rows = [];
  for (let i = 0; i < runs; i++) {
    const chrome = await chromeLauncher.launch({ chromePath, chromeFlags: ["--headless=new"] });
    const r = await lighthouse(url, { port: chrome.port, onlyCategories: ["performance"], output: "json", logLevel: "error" });
    await chrome.kill();
    const a = r.lhr.audits;
    rows.push({
      score: Math.round(r.lhr.categories.performance.score * 100),
      lcp: a["largest-contentful-paint"].numericValue,
      cls: a["cumulative-layout-shift"].numericValue,
      tbt: a["total-blocking-time"].numericValue,
      fcp: a["first-contentful-paint"].numericValue,
      lcpElement: a["largest-contentful-paint-element"]?.details?.items?.[0]?.items?.[0]?.node?.snippet ?? "",
    });
    if (i === runs - 1) fs.writeFileSync(`lh-${label}-${new URL(url).pathname.replace(/\W+/g, "_")}.json`, JSON.stringify(r.lhr));
  }
  out[url] = {
    runs: rows.map((r) => `${r.score}/${(r.lcp / 1000).toFixed(2)}s/${r.cls.toFixed(3)}/${Math.round(r.tbt)}ms`),
    median: { score: median(rows.map((r) => r.score)), lcpS: +(median(rows.map((r) => r.lcp)) / 1000).toFixed(2), cls: +median(rows.map((r) => r.cls)).toFixed(3), tbtMs: Math.round(median(rows.map((r) => r.tbt))), fcpS: +(median(rows.map((r) => r.fcp)) / 1000).toFixed(2) },
    lcpElement: rows[rows.length - 1].lcpElement,
  };
}
console.log(JSON.stringify({ label, out }, null, 1));
EOF
ls "$SP/tools/node_modules" | grep chrome-launcher
```

## Claude · 2026-11-23T18:32:31.932Z

The demo-banner `<p>` (weight 500) is the Store's LCP element. To choose the two weights to preload, I'll measure which weights render text in the first viewport at 390 and 1440 px.

**Tool: Bash**

```
$ SP="C:/Users/[USER]/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/27133936c4ab5668/27828732-4b43-4175-8312-75c91eb74045/scratchpad"; cat > "$SP/tools/weights.mjs" <<'EOF'
// Scratchpad-only: which font weights paint text inside the first viewport (chars per weight).
import puppeteer from "puppeteer-core";
const base = process.argv[2];
const browser = await puppeteer.launch({ executablePath: "C:/Program Files (x86)/Microsoft/Edge/Application/msedge.exe", headless: "new" });
for (const [w, h] of [[390, 844], [1440, 900]]) {
  for (const path of ["/", "/store", "/product/grand-theft-auto-v-steam-global"]) {
    const page = await browser.newPage();
    await page.setViewport({ width: w, height: h });
    await page.goto(base + path, { waitUntil: "networkidle0" });
    const res = await page.evaluate(() => {
      const out = {};
      const walker = document.createTreeWalker(document.body, NodeFilter.SHOW_TEXT);
      while (walker.nextNode()) {
        const n = walker.currentNode; const t = n.textContent.trim(); if (!t) continue;
        const el = n.parentElement; const r = el.getBoundingClientRect();
        if (r.bottom <= 0 || r.top >= innerHeight || r.width === 0) continue;
        const cs = getComputedStyle(el); if (cs.visibility === "hidden" || el.closest("[aria-hidden=true],[hidden]")) continue;
        if (r.width <= 1 && r.height <= 1) continue; // visually hidden
        out[cs.fontWeight] = (out[cs.fontWeight] || 0) + t.length;
      }
      return out;
    });
    console.log(w, path, JSON.stringify(res));
    await page.close();
  }
}
await browser.close();
EOF
cd "$SP/tools" && node weights.mjs http://127.0.0.1:4197
```

```
import styles from "./Skeleton.module.css";

interface SkeletonProps {
  /** text = one line of text (full width, one line tall); block = fills its box, the caller sets the size. */
  variant?: "text" | "block";
  /** Use it to set the size of a block, e.g. a portrait cover box. */
  className?: string;
```

```
// Route code splitting (Task 17). Home, Store and Product are in the main bundle: they are where visitors land,
// so their code must be there at once. The pages below are reached later (after a click), so each one is a separate
// JS file ("chunk") that the browser downloads only when it is needed - or earlier, when a link to it is hovered
// (PrefetchLink calls the same loader, see prefetch.ts).
//
// A loader is just a dynamic import(). The bundler (Vite) turns every import() into its own chunk, and the browser
// keeps a module it has loaded once: calling the same loader again costs nothing. That is why routes.ts (React.lazy)
// and the hover prefetch can both call these functions without downloading anything twice.

export const pageLoaders = {
  cart: () => import("../pages/CartPage.tsx"),
  checkout: () => import("../pages/CheckoutPage.tsx"),
  orders: () => import("../pages/OrdersPage.tsx"),
  order: () => import("../pages/OrderPage.tsx"),
  login: () => import("../pages/LoginPage.tsx"),
  register: () => import("../pages/RegisterPage.tsx"),
};

export type LazyPage = keyof typeof pageLoaders;

/** Tests: loads every lazy page up front, so a test never waits for a module to be transformed mid-render. */
export function loadAllPages(): Promise<unknown> {
  return Promise.all(Object.values(pageLoaders).map((load) => load()));
}
```

```
// The route table: which component shows for which URL. It is a plain array (not JSX) so tests can
// build a memory router from the very same routes that the real app uses (see main.tsx).

import type { RouteObject } from "react-router";

import { Layout } from "./Layout.tsx";
import { pageLoaders } from "./lazyPages.ts";
import { RouteError } from "./RouteError.tsx";

// Task 17: these pages are loaded on demand (code splitting, see lazyPages.ts). React.lazy wants a module with a
// `default` export; our pages use named exports, so `.then` picks the component. While a chunk is downloading the
// page shows the skeleton of the <Suspense> in Layout.tsx.
const CartPage = lazy(() => pageLoaders.cart().then((m) => ({ default: m.CartPage })));
const CheckoutPage = lazy(() => pageLoaders.checkout().then((m) => ({ default: m.CheckoutPage })));
const OrdersPage = lazy(() => pageLoaders.orders().then((m) => ({ default: m.OrdersPage })));
const OrderPage = lazy(() => pageLoaders.order().then((m) => ({ default: m.OrderPage })));
const LoginPage = lazy(() => pageLoaders.login().then((m) => ({ default: m.LoginPage })));
const RegisterPage = lazy(() => pageLoaders.register().then((m) => ({ default: m.RegisterPage })));

export const routes: RouteObject[] = [
  {
    // Layout route: renders the frame; the matching child is drawn at its <Outlet />.
    path: "/",
    Component: Layout,

    ErrorBoundary: AppError,

        ErrorBoundary: RouteError,
```

```
import type { ReactNode } from "react";
import { Button } from "./Button.tsx";
import styles from "./ErrorState.module.css";

interface ErrorStateProps {
  title?: string;
  /** Heading level of the title: h2 inside a page (default), h1 when this block IS the whole page (the error page). */
  as?: "h1" | "h2";
  /** Always a GENERIC sentence. Never pass a raw server, database or exception message here. */
  message?: string;
  /** Shows the Retry button when given. */
  onRetry?: () => void;
  /** True while the retry is running: the button shows its spinner. */
  retrying?: boolean;
  /** More actions next to Retry (the error page adds "Home"). */
  children?: ReactNode;
}

/** "Something went wrong" block with a Retry button (DESIGN.md 4.9). */
export function ErrorState({
  title = "Something went wrong",
  as: Heading = "h2",
  message = "Please try again in a moment.",
  onRetry,
  retrying = false,
  children,
}: ErrorStateProps) {
  return (
    <div className={styles.state} role="alert">
      <Heading className={styles.title}>{title}</Heading>
      <p className={styles.message}>{message}</p>
      {(onRetry || children) && (
        <div className={styles.actions}>
          {onRetry && (
            <Button onClick={onRetry} loading={retrying}>
              Retry
            </Button>
          )}
          {children}
        </div>
      )}
    </div>
  );
}
```

```
import { useEffect } from "react";
import { useRouteError } from "react-router";
import { ButtonLink } from "../components/Button.tsx";
import { ErrorState } from "../components/ErrorState.tsx";
import { useDocumentTitle } from "../lib/useDocumentTitle.ts";

/**
 * The route-level error page (React Router `ErrorBoundary`, routes.ts): shown INSIDE the layout when a page crashes
 * while rendering (a bug in our code, or a page chunk that could not be downloaded). The header, the footer and the
 * "Waking up the server…" notice stay; only the page area is replaced, so the app never goes blank.
 *  - Retry reloads the page: a fresh start fixes a failed chunk download (e.g. after a new deploy) or a one-off glitch.
 *  - Home goes to the home page; React Router clears the error as soon as the URL changes.
 * The real error goes to the browser console for the developer; the visitor only sees a generic message.
 */
export function RouteError() {
  const error = useRouteError();
  useDocumentTitle("Something went wrong");
  useEffect(() => {
    console.error(error);
  }, [error]);

  return (
    <ErrorState as="h1" message="This page could not be shown. Please try again." onRetry={() => window.location.reload()}>
      <ButtonLink to="/" variant="secondary">
        Home
      </ButtonLink>
    </ErrorState>
  );
}
```

```
import { useEffect } from "react";
import { useRouteError } from "react-router";
import { ButtonLink } from "../components/Button.tsx";
import { ErrorState } from "../components/ErrorState.tsx";
import { APP_NAME } from "../lib/appInfo.ts";
import { useDocumentTitle } from "../lib/useDocumentTitle.ts";
import styles from "./AppError.module.css";

/**
 * The app-level error page (the root route's `ErrorBoundary`, routes.ts). It is only reached when the FRAME itself
 * (header, footer, layout) crashes - a page crash is caught earlier by RouteError, inside the layout. Because the
 * frame is broken, this page draws its own minimal one, and "Home" reloads the whole document (`reloadDocument`)
 * instead of navigating inside the app that just failed.
 */
export function AppError() {
  const error = useRouteError();
  useDocumentTitle("Something went wrong");
  useEffect(() => {
    console.error(error);
  }, [error]);

  return (
    <main className={styles.page}>
      <p className={styles.wordmark}>{APP_NAME}</p>
      <ErrorState as="h1" message="The app could not be shown. Please try again." onRetry={() => window.location.reload()}>
        <ButtonLink to="/" variant="secondary" reloadDocument>
          Home
        </ButtonLink>
      </ErrorState>
    </main>
  );
}
```

```
import styles from "./PageSkeleton.module.css";
import { Skeleton } from "./Skeleton.tsx";
import { VisuallyHidden } from "./VisuallyHidden.tsx";

/**
 * The page area while a lazily loaded page's code downloads (Task 17, the <Suspense> fallback in Layout.tsx):
 * a title bar and a panel of lines, no "Loading…" text on screen (DESIGN.md 4.10). It is at least one screen tall,
 * so the footer stays below the fold and does not jump when the real page arrives.
 */
export function PageSkeleton() {
  return (
    <div className={styles.page} aria-busy="true">
      <VisuallyHidden>Loading the page…</VisuallyHidden>
      <Skeleton className={styles.title} />
      <div className={styles.panel}>
        <Skeleton />
        <Skeleton />
        <Skeleton />
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}
```

```
import { useEffect, useRef, useState } from "react";
import { useLocation } from "react-router";
import { VisuallyHidden } from "../components/VisuallyHidden.tsx";

/**
 * SPA accessibility (Task 17). In a normal website every click loads a new document: the browser puts the focus at
 * the top and a screen reader reads the new title. A single-page app only swaps part of the page, so by default the
 * focus stays on the clicked link (which may not exist any more) and nothing is announced. This component does what
 * the browser would do, after every change of PATH:
 *  - moves the focus to the new page's <h1> (or to <main> if the page has none yet, e.g. while it loads), so the next
 *    Tab continues from the page content and a screen reader starts reading there;
 *  - announces the new document title in a polite live region ("Cart – EneHomeworkBa").
 * Only the path counts: a Store filter changes the query string, and the focus must stay on the checkbox then.
 * The first render is skipped (on a fresh load the browser already does the right thing).
 * `preventScroll`: the scroll position is ScrollRestoration's job (Back returns to the same place in the list).
 */
export function RouteAnnouncer() {
  const { pathname } = useLocation();
  const [announcement, setAnnouncement] = useState("");
  const previousPath = useRef(pathname);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (previousPath.current === pathname) return; // first render, or the same path again
    previousPath.current = pathname;
    // One frame later: the new page has painted and its own effect has set document.title.
    const frame = requestAnimationFrame(() => {
      const main = document.getElementById("main");
      const target = main?.querySelector<HTMLElement>("h1") ?? main;
      if (target) {
        // A heading is not focusable by itself; tabindex -1 makes it focusable by script only (not by Tab).
        if (!target.hasAttribute("tabindex")) target.setAttribute("tabindex", "-1");
        target.focus({ preventScroll: true });
      }
      setAnnouncement(document.title);
    });
    return () => cancelAnimationFrame(frame);
  }, [pathname]);

  return (
    <div role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true">
      <VisuallyHidden>{announcement}</VisuallyHidden>
    </div>
  );
}
```

```
---
import { Suspense } from "react";
import { Outlet, ScrollRestoration } from "react-router";
import { PageSkeleton } from "../components/PageSkeleton.tsx";
import { DemoBanner } from "./DemoBanner.tsx";
import { Footer } from "./Footer.tsx";
import { Header } from "./Header.tsx";
import styles from "./Layout.module.css";
import { RouteAnnouncer } from "./RouteAnnouncer.tsx";
import { SlowServerNotice } from "./SlowServerNotice.tsx";
import { Toaster } from "./Toaster.tsx";

/**
 * The frame around every page. It is a "layout route": routes.ts nests all pages inside it, and
 * React Router puts the page of the current URL where <Outlet /> is. So the banner, header and footer
 * stay mounted while the pages change (the search text and focus are not lost on navigation).
 */
export function Layout() {
  return (
    <div className={styles.layout}>
      {/* The first thing Tab reaches: jumps over the banner and the header straight to the page (WCAG 2.4.1). */}
      <a href="#main" className={styles.skipLink}>
        Skip to content
      </a>
      <DemoBanner />
      <Header />
      <main id="main" tabIndex={-1} className={styles.main}>
        {/* Lazily loaded pages (Task 17, lazyPages.ts) show the skeleton while their code downloads. React Router
            changes pages inside startTransition, so after a CLICK the old page stays until the new one is ready;
            the skeleton appears when such a page is opened directly (typed URL, reload). */}
        <Suspense fallback={<PageSkeleton />}>
          <Outlet />
        </Suspense>
      </main>
      <Footer />
      {/* After the page: its effects (document title) have run when this one moves the focus. */}
      <RouteAnnouncer />
      {/* Bottom corner: the toast ("Added to cart") and the "waking up" notice, stacked so they never overlap. */}
      <div className={styles.notices}>
        <Toaster />
        <SlowServerNotice />
      </div>
      {/* Remembers the scroll position of every history entry: Back from a product returns to the same place in
          the Store. A new page opens at the top (filter changes opt out, see useStoreParams). */}
      <ScrollRestoration />
    </div>
  );
}
```

```
// Prefetch on intent (Task 17): when the user is ABOUT to open a page (pointer resting on a link, keyboard focus on it,
// finger touching it), start downloading what that page needs. By the time the click lands, the data is usually in the
// TanStack Query cache and the page's code is loaded, so the page opens with no loading state at all.
//
// This file decides WHAT to prefetch for a link target (planPrefetch, a pure function) and does it (prefetchRoute).
// WHEN is decided by PrefetchLink (src/components/PrefetchLink.tsx).
import type { QueryClient } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { matchPath, type To } from "react-router";
import { orderListQuery, productListQuery, productQuery, queryKeys } from "../api/queries.ts";
import { parseStoreParams, type StoreParams } from "../lib/storeParams.ts";
import { pageLoaders, type LazyPage } from "./lazyPages.ts";

/** How long the pointer must rest on a link before it counts as intent (a pointer passing over it costs nothing). */
export const HOVER_DELAY_MS = 80;

/**
 * A prefetched answer counts as fresh this long: within it, hovering the same link again sends no request.
 * It is the app's default staleTime (queryClient.ts). The order list's own staleTime is 0 (statuses change), so without
 * this every hover over "My orders" would ask again; its page still refreshes the list when it opens.
 */
export const PREFETCH_FRESH_MS = 30_000;

/** The data a page shows first, as plain data (easy to test); prefetchRoute turns it into the page's own query. */
export type PrefetchData =
  | { kind: "product"; slug: string }
  | { kind: "products"; params: StoreParams }
  | { kind: "orders" };

export interface PrefetchPlan {
  /** The lazily loaded page whose code to download (lazyPages.ts); none for the pages in the main bundle. */
  page?: LazyPage;
  data?: PrefetchData;
}

/** Splits a link target into path + query string. Only this app's own paths ("/...") are prefetched. */
function splitTarget(to: To): { pathname: string; search: string } | null {
  if (typeof to === "string") {
    // "//host/x" would be another site; anything not starting with "/" is relative or external.
    if (!to.startsWith("/") || to.startsWith("//")) return null;
    const url = new URL(to, "http://app.invalid"); // the base only lets URL parse a path; it is never used
    return { pathname: url.pathname, search: url.search };
  }
```

```
---
import { useQueryClient } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { useEffect, useRef, type ComponentProps } from "react";
import { Link } from "react-router";
import { HOVER_DELAY_MS, prefetchRoute } from "../app/prefetch.ts";

export type PrefetchLinkProps = ComponentProps<typeof Link>;

/**
 * The app's internal link (Task 17): a normal React Router <Link> that starts loading the target page when the user
 * shows the intent to open it (see src/app/prefetch.ts for WHAT is loaded):
 *  - pointer resting on it for HOVER_DELAY_MS (80 ms) - leaving earlier cancels, so sweeping the mouse across a grid
 *    of cards does not fire a request per card;
 *  - keyboard focus - Tab reaching a link is a strong sign, so no delay;
 *  - touchstart - phones have no hover; the finger touches ~100 ms before the click fires, enough to start early.
 * Every handler the caller passes (onMouseEnter, onFocus...) still runs.
 */
export function PrefetchLink({ to, onMouseEnter, onMouseLeave, onFocus, onTouchStart, ...rest }: PrefetchLinkProps) {
  const queryClient = useQueryClient();
  const hoverTimer = useRef<ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>>(undefined);

  // A link that disappears while the pointer is on it must not prefetch afterwards.
  useEffect(() => () => clearTimeout(hoverTimer.current), []);

  const prefetch = () => prefetchRoute(queryClient, to);

  return (
    <Link
      {...rest}
      to={to}
      onMouseEnter={(event) => {
        onMouseEnter?.(event);
        clearTimeout(hoverTimer.current);
        hoverTimer.current = setTimeout(prefetch, HOVER_DELAY_MS);
      }}
      onMouseLeave={(event) => {
        onMouseLeave?.(event);
        clearTimeout(hoverTimer.current);
      }}
      onFocus={(event) => {
        onFocus?.(event);
        prefetch();
      }}
      onTouchStart={(event) => {
        onTouchStart?.(event);
        prefetch();
      }}
    />
  );
}
```

```
$ cat > "C:/Users/[USER]/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/27133936c4ab5668/27828732-4b43-4175-8312-75c91eb74045/scratchpad/swap-links.mjs" <<'EOF'
// Scratchpad-only: replaces react-router <Link> with <PrefetchLink> in the given files (import + tags).
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
for (const file of process.argv.slice(2)) {
  let src = fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8");
  const rel = path.relative(path.dirname(file), "src/components/PrefetchLink.tsx").replaceAll("\\", "/");
  const spec = rel.startsWith(".") ? rel : "./" + rel;
  src = src.replace(/import \{([^}]*)\} from "react-router";\n/, (m, names) => {
    const rest = names.split(",").map((s) => s.trim()).filter((s) => s && s !== "Link");
    const rr = rest.length ? `import { ${rest.join(", ")} } from "react-router";\n` : "";
    return rr + `import { PrefetchLink } from "${spec}";\n`;
  });
  src = src.replaceAll("<Link\n", "<PrefetchLink\n").replaceAll("<Link ", "<PrefetchLink ").replaceAll("</Link>", "</PrefetchLink>");
  fs.writeFileSync(file, src);
  console.log("swapped", file, spec);
}
EOF
```

```
// skips the orders for a signed-out visitor, and does nothing at all with Data Saver on.
```

```
// @vitest-environment jsdom
// WHEN PrefetchLink prefetches: after the pointer rests 80 ms (not when it only passes over), at once on keyboard focus
// and on touchstart; the caller's own handlers still run. WHAT it prefetches is tested in src/app/prefetch.test.ts.
import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { act } from "react";
import { MemoryRouter } from "react-router";

  vi.useFakeTimers();
  fetchMock = vi.fn(async () => new Response("{}", { status: 200 }));
  vi.stubGlobal("fetch", fetchMock);
});

  cleanup?.();
  vi.useRealTimers();
  vi.unstubAllGlobals();
});

function mount(extra: Partial<React.ComponentProps<typeof PrefetchLink>> = {}) {

          The Witcher 3
        </PrefetchLink>
      </MemoryRouter>
    </QueryClientProvider>,
  );
  cleanup = view.unmount;
  return view.container.querySelector("a")!;
}

const pointerOut = (link: Element) => fire(link, new MouseEvent("mouseout", { bubbles: true }));

    wait(HOVER_DELAY_MS - 1);
    expect(requested()).toEqual([]);
    wait(1);
    expect(requested()).toEqual(["/api/products/the-witcher-3"]);
```

```
// SPA accessibility (Task 17): after a change of PATH the focus moves to the new page's h1 and the new title is

    </QueryClientProvider>,
  );
  cleanup = view.unmount;
  return router;
}
```

```
};
```

<details><summary>Result</summary>

```
/*
 * Block slots on the home page grid (HomePage.module.css: two columns, "1fr auto"). Every block takes the
 * full width - except that on desktop the hero and the tiles that FOLLOW it share one row, like Eneba's banner
 * row: the carousel on the left, the tiles on the right. If either is switched off, the other is simply full
 * width; the page needs no code for that.
 */
.slot {
  grid-column: 1 / -1;
  min-width: 0;
}

.hero:has(+ .tiles) {
  grid-column: 1;
}

/* Next to the carousel the tiles are narrow and as tall as the hero (SideTiles reads these two variables). */
.hero + .tiles {
  grid-column: 2;
  --tile-width: var(--hero-tile-width);
  --tile-height: var(--hero-height);
}

/* Every second PRODUCT ROW sits on a darker full-bleed band, like Eneba's "Top games" (counted among the
   product rows only, so switching another block off does not change the stripes). */
.slot:nth-child(2n of [data-block-type="product_row"]) {
  background: var(--color-band);
}

/* Tablets and phones: the tiles go under the carousel, full width (the variables fall back to their defaults). */
@media (max-width: 930px) {
  .hero:has(+ .tiles),
  .hero + .tiles {
    grid-column: 1 / -1;
  }

  .hero + .tiles {
    --tile-width: initial;
    --tile-height: initial;
  }
}
```

```
// Scratchpad-only: rendered width of the hero <img> and the file the browser picked, per viewport.
import puppeteer from "puppeteer-core";
const browser = await puppeteer.launch({ executablePath: "C:/Program Files (x86)/Microsoft/Edge/Application/msedge.exe", headless: "new" });
for (const [w, dpr] of [[1440, 1], [1920, 1], [1200, 1], [1024, 1], [930, 1], [768, 2], [390, 3]]) {
  const page = await browser.newPage();
  await page.setViewport({ width: w, height: 900, deviceScaleFactor: dpr });
  await page.goto(process.argv[2] + "/", { waitUntil: "networkidle2" });
  await page.waitForSelector("section[aria-roledescription=carousel] img");
  const r = await page.evaluate(() => {
    const img = document.querySelector("section[aria-roledescription=carousel] img");
    return { width: Math.round(img.getBoundingClientRect().width), vw: innerWidth, src: img.currentSrc.split("/").pop() };
  });
  console.log(w, "dpr", dpr, JSON.stringify(r), "width/vw =", (r.width / r.vw).toFixed(2));
  await page.close();
}
await browser.close();
EOF
cd "$SP/tools" && node herosize.mjs http://127.0.0.1:4198
```

```
// fakeContent stands in for the Store. It holds only ENABLED rows, like the real Store returns:
// a disabled block is simply not in `blocks`.
type fakeContent struct {
	blocks []Block
	slides []Slide
	tiles  []Tile
	err    error
}

func (f *fakeContent) Blocks(context.Context) ([]Block, error) { return f.blocks, f.err }

func (f *fakeContent) BlockByKey(_ context.Context, key string) (Block, error) {
	if f.err != nil {
		return Block{}, f.err
	}
	for _, b := range f.blocks {
		if b.Key == key {
			return b, nil
		}
	}
	return Block{}, ErrNotFound
}

func (f *fakeContent) BlockByType(_ context.Context, blockType string) (Block, error) {
	if f.err != nil {
		return Block{}, f.err
	}
	for _, b := range f.blocks {
		if b.Type == blockType {
			return b, nil
		}
	}
	return Block{}, ErrNotFound
}

func (f *fakeContent) Tiles(context.Context) ([]Tile, error)   { return f.tiles, f.err }

	products map[string]catalog.ProductDetail
	page     catalog.Page
	filters  []catalog.Filter
	err      error
}

func (f *fakeCatalog) ListProducts(_ context.Context, flt catalog.Filter) (catalog.Page, error) {
```

```
import { useSyncExternalStore } from "react";

const QUERY = "(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)";

/** The media query list, or null where matchMedia does not exist (old browsers, some test environments). */
function mediaQuery(): MediaQueryList | null {
  return typeof window !== "undefined" && typeof window.matchMedia === "function" ? window.matchMedia(QUERY) : null;
}

function subscribe(onChange: () => void) {
  const mql = mediaQuery();
  mql?.addEventListener("change", onChange);
  return () => mql?.removeEventListener("change", onChange);
}

/**
 * True when the user asked the operating system to reduce motion. useSyncExternalStore keeps it LIVE:
 * switching the setting while the page is open updates every component that uses it.
 * CSS animations are already switched off globally (global.css); this is for motion started by JavaScript,
 * like the hero carousel's autoplay.
 */
export function usePrefersReducedMotion(): boolean {
  return useSyncExternalStore(subscribe, () => mediaQuery()?.matches ?? false);
}
src/features/home/HeroCarousel.test.tsx:23:/** A fake matchMedia for "(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)"; `set` flips it like the OS setting would. */
src/features/home/HeroCarousel.test.tsx:32:  window.matchMedia = vi.fn(() => mql) as never;
```

Same guard pattern then. Writing the shared hero image helper and wiring it in:

**Tool: Write**

```
c:\PROJEKTAI\ENEBA\ENEBA-homework\src\features\home\heroImage.ts
---
// The hero picture's responsive-image settings in ONE place (Task 17): the carousel's <picture> uses them, and so does
// the early preload. A preload is only reused when it asks for exactly the file the <img> will pick, so both must
// describe the picture the same way.
import { preload } from "react-dom";
import type { SlideImage } from "../../api/types.ts";

/** Phones get the phone crop of a slide (the <source> of the <picture>). Same width as the CSS breakpoint. */
export const HERO_PHONE_MEDIA = "(max-width: 600px)";
/** The phone crop is drawn across the whole screen. */
export const HERO_PHONE_SIZES = "100vw";
/**
 * How wide the desktop picture is drawn, so the browser can pick the smallest sharp file before the layout exists:
 * the full width up to 930 px; above it the carousel shares its row with the side tiles. Measured: at 1440 px it is
 * exactly 70vw (1008 px). The tiles are server content (they can be switched off), so no fixed calc() is right in
 * every case; 70vw picks the same file as the real width at every viewport checked (Task 17 report).
 */
export const HERO_SIZES = "(max-width: 930px) 100vw, 70vw";

/**
 * Starts downloading the first slide's picture - the biggest thing on the first screen (the page's LCP element) - as
 * soon as GET /api/homepage has named it, instead of after GET /api/blocks/hero answers. React's preload() adds a
 * <link rel="preload" as="image" imagesrcset=... fetchpriority="high"> to <head> (once per picture, however often it
 * is called). The crop is chosen with the same media query as the <picture>, so only ONE file is downloaded.
 */
export function preloadHeroImage(image: SlideImage): void {
  // matchMedia is missing in some test environments: then the desktop crop is assumed.
  const phone = typeof window.matchMedia === "function" && window.matchMedia(HERO_PHONE_MEDIA).matches;
  const href = phone ? image.mobileUrl : image.url;
  const srcset = (phone ? image.mobileSrcset : image.srcset) ?? undefined;
  preload(href, {
    as: "image",
    fetchPriority: "high",
    imageSrcSet: srcset,
    imageSizes: srcset ? (phone ? HERO_PHONE_SIZES : HERO_SIZES) : undefined,
  });
}
```

```
/*
 * Hero carousel (DESIGN.md 3.4). Desktop / tablet: a fixed 288px tall box, the picture fills it (object-fit: cover)
 * and the text sits on the left over a dark scrim. Phones: a 16:9 box, the text at the bottom over a bottom scrim.
 * The box has its size BEFORE any picture loads, so nothing on the page moves (CLS).
 */
.carousel {
  position: relative;
  height: var(--hero-height);
  overflow: hidden;
  background: var(--color-surface-card);
}

.skeleton {
  width: 100%;
  height: 100%;
}

.errorFrame {
  display: grid;
  place-items: center;
  padding: var(--space-4);
}

/* All slides share one grid cell (stacked); only the active one is visible. A cross-fade, not a slide-in:
   the box never changes size and nothing outside it moves. */
.slides {
  display: grid;
  /* One cell exactly the size of the box: an "auto" row would grow to the tallest picture and be cut off. */
  grid-template: minmax(0, 1fr) / minmax(0, 1fr);
  height: 100%;
}

.slide {
  position: relative;
  grid-area: 1 / 1;
  min-width: 0;
  min-height: 0;
  opacity: 0;
  visibility: hidden;
  transition:
    opacity var(--slide-transition),
    visibility var(--slide-transition);
}

.active {
  opacity: 1;
  visibility: visible;
}

.picture,
.image {
  width: 100%;
  height: 100%;
}

.image {
  object-fit: cover;
}

/* The text column, over the scrim that keeps white text readable on light art. */
.content {
  position: absolute;
  inset: 0;
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  justify-content:
```

```
sub(p,""" * While loading, the skeleton has exactly the carousel's size, so nothing moves when the slides arrive (CLS).
 */
export function HeroCarousel({ block }: BlockProps) {
  const query = useQuery(homeBlockQuery<HeroBlockData>(block.endpoint));

  if (query.isPending) {
    return (
      <div className={styles.carousel} aria-hidden="true">
        <Skeleton variant="block" className={styles.skeleton} />
      </div>
    );
  }""",""" * While loading, the placeholder has exactly the carousel's size, so nothing moves when the slides arrive (CLS).
 */
export function HeroCarousel({ block }: BlockProps) {
  const query = useQuery(homeBlockQuery<HeroBlockData>(block.endpoint));
  // Task 17 (LCP): the registry (already in the cache - this block exists because the registry listed it) names the
  // first slide's picture, and HomePage has started downloading it. While the slides load, that picture is shown in
  // the placeholder: the biggest thing on the screen appears as soon as it is downloaded, not after this block's
  // request. Read from the cache, not with useQuery: this must never send a request of its own.
  const queryClient = useQueryClient();
  const firstImage = queryClient.getQueryData<HomepageResponse>(queryKeys.homepage)?.preload?.heroImage;

  if (query.isPending) {
    return (
      <div className={styles.carousel} aria-hidden="true">
        {firstImage ? <HeroPicture image={firstImage} first /> : <Skeleton variant="block" className={styles.skeleton} />}
      </div>
    );
```

```
/** A fake matchMedia for "(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)"; `set` flips it like the OS setting would. */
function fakeReducedMotion(matches: boolean) {
```

```
export const AUTOPLAY_MS = 6000;

/** A finger must move at least this far sideways for a swipe (a small wobble while tapping a button is not one). */
export const SWIPE_MIN_PX = 40;

/**
 * Task 17: what a finger movement means. A swipe is mostly HORIZONTAL (more sideways than up/down) and long enough;
 * to the left shows the next slide, to the right the previous one (the content follows the finger).
 * Anything else - a tap, a short wobble, a vertical scroll - is not a swipe (null).
 */
export function swipeDirection(dx: number, dy: number): "next" | "prev" | null {
  if (Math.abs(dx) < SWIPE_MIN_PX || Math.abs(dx) <= Math.abs(dy)) return null;
  return dx < 0 ? "next" : "prev";
}
```

Neither server logs requests, so I'll count on the page side instead, by wrapping `window.fetch` before the app loads:

**Tool: Bash**

```
$ SP="C:/Users/[USER]/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/27133936c4ab5668/27828732-4b43-4175-8312-75c91eb74045/scratchpad"; cat > "$SP/tools/touchcount.mjs" <<'EOF'
// Scratchpad-only: how many fetch() calls the page itself makes for the product after a touchstart.
import puppeteer from "puppeteer-core";
const base = process.argv[2];
const browser = await puppeteer.launch({ executablePath: "C:/Program Files (x86)/Microsoft/Edge/Application/msedge.exe", headless: "new" });
const page = await browser.newPage();
await page.emulate({ viewport: { width: 390, height: 844, deviceScaleFactor: 3, isMobile: true, hasTouch: true }, userAgent: "Mozilla/5.0 (Linux; Android 14) AppleWebKit/537.36 (KHTML, like Gecko) Chrome/130 Mobile Safari/537.36" });
await page.evaluateOnNewDocument(() => {
  window.__fetches = [];
  const orig = window.fetch;
  window.fetch = (input, init) => { window.__fetches.push(String(input)); return orig(input, init); };
});
let cdp = 0;
page.on("request", (r) => { if (r.url().includes("/api/products/")) cdp++; });
await page.goto(base + "/store", { waitUntil: "load" });
await page.waitForSelector("main ul a[href^='/product/']");
const box = await (await page.$("main ul a[href^='/product/']")).boundingBox();
await page.touchscreen.touchStart(box.x + 20, box.y + 20);
await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 400));
const fetches = await page.evaluate(() => window.__fetches.filter((u) => u.startsWith("/api/products/")));
console.log("fetch() calls by the page:", fetches, "| request events seen by the protocol:", cdp);
await browser.close();
EOF
```

```
import puppeteer from "puppeteer-core";
```

```
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
const results = [];

async function settle(page) {
  // every image in the first screen loaded, fonts ready, no skeleton left
  await page.waitForFunction(() => document.fonts.status === "loaded" && !document.querySelector("main [aria-busy=true]"), { timeout: 15000 }).catch(() => {});
  await page.waitForFunction(() => [...document.images].filter((i) => { const r = i.getBoundingClientRect(); return r.top < innerHeight && r.bottom > 0 && r.width > 0; }).every((i) => i.complete), { timeout: 15000 }).catch(() => {});
  await sleep(600);
}
async function check(page, name, size) {
  await settle(page);
  await page.addScriptTag({ path: axePath });
  const v = await page.evaluate(async () => {
    const r = await window.axe.run(document, { runOnly: ["wcag2a", "wcag2aa", "wcag21a", "wcag21aa", "best-practice"] });
    return r.violations.map((x) => `${x.id} (${x.nodes.length}): ${x.nodes[0].target.join(" ")}`);
  });
  const title = await page.title();
  await page.screenshot({ path: `${outDir}/${name}-${size}.png` });
  results.push({ name, size, title, axe: v.length, violations: v });
  console.log(size, name.padEnd(13), "axe:", v.length, v.join(" | "), "|", title);
}
async function clickText(page, selector, text) {
  const ok = await page.evaluate((selector, text) => {
    const el = [...document.querySelectorAll(selector)].find((e) => e.textContent.trim().startsWith(text) && e.offsetParent !== null);
    if (el) el.click();
    return !!el;
  }, selector, text);
  if (!ok) throw new Error(`no ${selector} "${text}"`);
}
```

```
import puppeteer from "puppeteer-core";
const out = process.argv[2];
const browser = await puppeteer.launch({ executablePath: "C:/Program Files (x86)/Microsoft/Edge/Application/msedge.exe", headless: "new" });
const pages = [["home", "https://www.eneba.com/"], ["store", "https://www.eneba.com/store/all"], ["store-search", "https://www.eneba.com/store/all?text=witcher"], ["404", "https://www.eneba.com/this-page-does-not-exist-xyz"]];

    await page.setViewport({ width: w, height: h, isMobile: size === "mobile", hasTouch: size === "mobile" });

    try {
      await page.goto(url, { waitUntil: "load", timeout: 45000 });
      await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 3500));
      const info = await page.evaluate(() => ({ title: document.title, h1: document.querySelector("h1")?.textContent?.slice(0, 60), skip: [...document.querySelectorAll("a")].some((a) => /skip/i.test(a.textContent)) }));
      await page.screenshot({ path: `${out}/${name}-${size}.png` });
      console.log(size, name, JSON.stringify(info));
    } catch (e) { console.log(size, name, "FAILED", e.message.slice(0, 80)); }
    await page.close();
  }
}
await browser.close();
EOF
```

```
  useDocumentTitle("Page not found");
  return (
    <EmptyState as="h1" title="Page not found" message="The page you are looking for does not exist or has moved.">
```

```
import { ButtonLink } from "../components/Button.tsx";
import { EmptyState } from "../components/EmptyState.tsx";
import { useDocumentTitle } from "../lib/useDocumentTitle.ts";

/** The catch-all route (path "*"): any URL that no other route matches. It still renders inside the layout. */
export function NotFoundPage() {
  useDocumentTitle("Page not found");
  return (
    <EmptyState as="h1" title="Page not found" message="The page you are looking for does not exist or has moved.">

}
```

```
- **Partial UNIQUE `payments_one_success_per_order (order_id) WHERE outcome = 'succeeded'`:** at most one successful payment per order – a double charge is impossible at the database level, whatever the Go code does.

### Change to `keys`
`ALTER TABLE keys ADD COLUMN order_item_id bigint REFERENCES order_items (id)` (RESTRICT) + the CHECK and the partial index described under [`keys`](#keys).
A nullable column added to an existing table → backward compatible (expand).

## Home page tables (created – migration `00006`, Task 15)

The home page is assembled from **blocks**. The registry (`homepage_blocks`) says which blocks the page shows, in which
order and with which titles; `GET /api/homepage` returns the enabled rows. Every block has its **own endpoint and data
source** (`pkg/homepage`): the hero reads `homepage_slides` (+ live prices from the catalog), the tiles read `homepage_tiles`
(+ cover images from the catalog), the platform row and the product rows read only the catalog. The frontend has one
renderer per block **type** and no content of its own.

**The content rows are inserted by the migration, not by `cmd/seed`** (owner decision): the catalog seed and its golden
checksum stay unchanged, and production needs no reseed (`seed --reset` refuses once orders exist). `seed --reset` only
deletes the catalog tables, so it never touches these.

**No foreign key into the catalog:** `homepage_slides.product_slug` is plain text. The blocks are logically separate
```

```
## How this project was built
Planning chat → task file → Claude Code works on a `task/NN-name` branch → PR with a report → review → merge.

- [`CLAUDE.md`](CLAUDE.md) – standing rules Claude Code follows in every session
- [`docs/tasks/`](docs/tasks/) – the task files, saved verbatim
- [`docs/reports/`](docs/reports/) – one report per task (what changed, how it was verified, open questions)
- [`ai-logs/`](ai-logs/) – exported Claude Code sessions

## About the author
**[Kodmo Lab](https://kodmolab.lt)** is a small software studio building practical, offline-first tools for small businesses.
Its main product, **[Kodmo Auto](https://kodmolab.lt/auto/)**, is an offline Windows app for auto repair shops in Lithuania
(customers, jobs, parts inventory, staff; low-stock alerts), built with Python, PySide6, FastAPI and SQLite and developed end to end
with AI coding tools (Claude Code) using the same small-task / spec / testing workflow as this project.

## Layout
- `api/index.go` – the only Vercel function; forwards to the router
- `pkg/server/` – router and handlers (they depend on small interfaces: `DBPinger`, `CatalogReader`, `HomepageReader`, ...); query-string validation
- `pkg/db/` – lazy, shared pgx connection pool
- `pkg/catalog/` – catalog queries (listing + facets, product page, search + suggestions), seller
```

```
- **Hero picture early (Task 17, LCP):** `GET /api/homepage` names the first shown slide's picture (`preload.heroImage`); the page preloads it
  (`<link rel="preload" as="image" imagesrcset imagesizes fetchpriority="high">`, the crop chosen with the same media query as the `<picture>`) and the
  hero's loading placeholder already draws it, so the slide text arrives on top of a picture that is already there. `sizes` = `(max-width: 930px) 100vw, 70vw`
  (`heroImage.ts`, shared by both): exact at 1440 (1008px); the true width above 930 is `100vw − tiles`, but the tiles are server content, and 70vw picks the
  same file as the real width at 1024, 1200, 1440 and 1920.""")
sub(p,"""- Inline (inside a small component, like a filter group): same copy, `--font-size-xs`, text button "Retry".""","""- Inline (inside a small component, like a filter group): same copy, `--font-size-xs`, text button "Retry".
- **Error page (Task 17):** a page that crashes while rendering (or whose code cannot be downloaded) shows the error state **as the page** (`h1`),
  inside the layout: "Something went wrong" + "This page could not be shown. Please try again." + primary **Retry** (reloads) + secondary **Home**.
  If the frame itself (header / footer) crashes, a full page of its own replaces it: the wordmark as text, the same block, Home reloads the document.
  The tab title is "Something went wrong". Never a white screen.
```

```
# Task 17 – greitis, atsparumas, prieinamumas, galutinė peržiūra

## Prefetch „pagal ketinimą" (`src/components/PrefetchLink.tsx`, `src/app/prefetch.ts`)
- **Ką daro:** `PrefetchLink` – įprasta React Router `<Link>`, kuri pradeda krauti tikslinį puslapį, kai vartotojas **parodo ketinimą** jį atidaryti: pelė **ilsisi** ant nuorodos 80 ms (`HOVER_DELAY_MS`; nuėjus anksčiau – atšaukiama), nuoroda gauna **fokusą** (Tab), arba pirštas jos **paliečia** (`touchstart`). Visos vidinės nuorodos (header, meniu, footer, kortelės, plytelės, platformos, „View all", „Other versions", breadcrumb'ai, užsakymai, `ButtonLink`) eina per ją.
- **Ką krauna (`planPrefetch`):** grynoji funkcija – iš adreso pasako planą: `/product/:slug` → produkto užklausa; `/store?...` → sąrašo užklausa su **tuo pačiu valytoju** (`parseStoreParams`), kurį naudoja Store puslapis, todėl kešo raktas lygiai tas pats; `/orders` → kodas + užsakymų sąrašas (**tik prisijungus**, kitaip serveris atsakytų 401); `/cart`, `/login`, `/register`, `/orders/:id`, `/checkout/:id` → tik puslapio kodas (chunk); `/` → namų puslapio registras; nežinomas adresas ar kita svetainė → nieko. `prefetchRoute` planą įvykdo.
- **Kodėl 80 ms:** braukiant pele per kortelių tinklelį pelė praeina per daug nuorodų – be delsos būtų užklausa kiekvienai. 80 ms – žmogui nepastebima, bet atsitiktinius praėjimus atfiltruoja. **Fokusas ir prisilietimas – be delsos:** jie jau yra stiprus ženklas. **Telefonuose nėra hover** – todėl `touchstart`: pirštas paliečia ~100 ms anksčiau nei įvyksta click.
- **Ne du kartus:** `prefetchQuery` nieko nedaro, kol atsakymas „šviežias" (`PREFETCH_FRESH_MS` = 30 s, kaip programos `staleTime`) arba jau keliauja. Užsakymų sąrašo `staleTime` yra 0, todėl be šio lango kiekvienas užvedimas ant „My orders" siųstų užklausą; pats puslapis atsidaręs vis tiek atnaujina sąrašą. Kodas: naršyklė modulį įsimena, antras `import()` nieko nesiunčia.
- **Data Saver (`saveDataOn`):** jei vartotojas naršyklėje įjungęs duomenų taupymą (`naviga
… [truncated; original 11252 chars]
```

```
// Scratchpad-only: re-take the 404 screenshots (+ axe) after the 404 button change.
import puppeteer from "puppeteer-core";
import { createRequire } from "node:module";
const require = createRequire(import.meta.url);

const browser = await puppeteer.launch({ executablePath: "C:/Program Files (x86)/Microsoft/Edge/Application/msedge.exe", headless: "new" });
for (const [w, h, size] of [[1440, 900, "desktop"], [390, 844, "mobile"]]) {
  const page = await browser.newPage();
  await page.setViewport({ width: w, height: h, isMobile: size === "mobile", hasTouch: size === "mobile" });
  await page.emulateMediaFeatures([{ name: "prefers-reduced-motion", value: "reduce" }]);
  await page.goto(base + "/nope/not/here", { waitUntil: "load" });
  await page.waitForSelector("main h1");
  await page.waitForFunction(() => document.fonts.status === "loaded");
  await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 600));
  await page.addScriptTag({ path: require.resolve("axe-core/axe.min.js") });
  const v = await page.evaluate(async () => (await window.axe.run(document, { runOnly: ["wcag2a", "wcag2aa", "wcag21a", "wcag21aa", "best-practice"] })).violations.length);
  await page.screenshot({ path: `${outDir}/404-${size}.png` });
  console.log(size, "404 axe:", v);
  await page.close();
}
await browser.close();
EOF
```

```
- `src/components/PrefetchLink.tsx` (new) – the app's internal link: hover ≥ 80 ms (cancelled on leave / unmount), focus, touchstart; the caller's own handlers still run.
- Every internal link now uses it: `Header`, `MenuDrawer`, `Footer`, `AccountMenu`, `Toaster` (action link), `Breadcrumb`, `ButtonLink` (`Button.tsx` – so "View all", the hero CTA, tiles' and empty states' buttons), `AuthPage` (switch link), `CartLineItem`, `CartView`, `PlatformRow`, `SideTiles`, `OrderList`, `OrderItemList`, `BuyBox`, `VariantList`, `SearchBox` ("See all results"), `SuggestionOption`, `ProductCard`, `NotFoundPage`.

- `src/main.tsx` – starts the opened URL's data request before the first render (`prefetchRoute(queryClient, location)`); `/` now plans the home registry.

- `src/components/PageSkeleton.tsx` + `.module.css` (new).

**Performance (decision 3 + addendum)**
- `index.html` – `preconnect` `https://images.igdb.com`; `preload` Metropolis 500 + 700 (Vite rewrites the paths to the hashed files).
```

```
---
## Summary
- **PrefetchLink** for every internal link: hover ≥ 80 ms / focus / touchstart prefetch the target's data (same TanStack queries, Store params through the Store's own sanitizer, orders only when signed in) and its code chunk; once per 30 s; off with Data Saver.
- **Code splitting:** cart, payment, orders, order, sign-in, sign-up are lazy routes with a skeleton; Home / Store / Product stay in the main bundle (main JS gzip 149.2 → 144.0 kB).
- **Performance:** the home registry now names the first hero picture (`preload.heroImage`), the page preloads it and the hero placeholder shows it; preconnect to IGDB, preload of the two above-the-fold font weights. Lighthouse mobile Home LCP **4.94 → 2.68 s**, CLS ≤ 0.002 everywhere; the 2.5 s target is not reliably met locally (Store 2.48–2.58 s) – conditions and runs in the report.
- **Resilience and accessibility:** error pages with Retry + Home (page crash inside the layout, frame crash as a full page, failed chunk download); focus + title announcement on every page change; unique tab titles; carousel swipe on touch screens; axe 0 on all 11 pages at 1440 and 390; our screenshots in `docs/screenshots/`.
- **No migration** (the registry JSON gains an optional field).

## Task and report
- Task file: [`docs/tasks/TASK_17_polish.md`](docs/tasks/TASK_17_polish.md)
- Report: [`docs/reports/TASK_17_polish.md`](docs/reports/TASK_17_polish.md)

## CI status
Local: `gofmt -l .` empty, `go build / vet / test ./...` pass, `npm run lint` clean, `npm test` 50 files / 625 tests pass, `npm run build` passes. GitHub checks: see below / the report.

## Manual test checklist
- [ ] Vercel Preview URL opens and the page loads
- [ ] `<preview-url>/api/health` returns `{"status":"ok","db":"ok",...}`
- [ ] Hover a Store card: Network shows `/api/products/<slug>` before the click; hover "Sign in": the login chunk loads
- [ ] Phone emulation: touching a card starts the request; the home carousel follows a swipe, vertical scrolling still works
- [ ] Open `/cart` directly: layout + skeleton, then the cart; after any click the focus is on the new page's heading
- [ ] Optional: Lighthouse mobile on the preview's `/` and `/store`
```
